ON THE TITLE DEFENSE
• James Madison returns home to take on Troy in a 2025 Sun Belt Championship Game rematch on Thursday 
night at Bridgeforth Stadium .
• JMU enters the game with a 29-8 mark in Sun Belt play since entering the league in 2022, including 15-3 at 
home . The Dukes also are 7-1 against West Division foes .
• In all, James Madison has won 12 of its last 13 regular-season Sun Belt games .
• JMU has the most wins of any Sun Belt team since 2022, with 47 . Troy is second with 38 victories .
• The Dukes are one of seven FBS programs to have won seven times this season, including one of only two 
Group of Six programs (UTSA) . They also are one of five one-loss G6 squads .
• JMU coach Billy Napier won four straight Sun Belt West Division titles at Louisiana from 2018-21 . He also led 
the Ragin’ Cajuns to SBC championships in 2020 and 2021 . 
• The Dukes went a perfect 8-0 in SBC play last season and entered the 2026 campaign as the East Division 
favorites, according to the Sun Belt’s preseason coaches poll .
ELEVEN STRAIGHT AT BRIDGEFORTH
• The Dukes enter Thursday’s contest on an 11-game home win streak at Bridgeforth Stadium .
• The streak is tied for the sixth-longest in the FBS of its kind . Ohio and North Dakota State have won 17 straight 
home games to lead the nation .
• JMU went a perfect 7-0 at home last season and is 4-0 in Harrisonburg this fall .
• Since 2016, JMU is 69-5 at home .
• The Dukes also are 26-3 at home since joining the FBS ranks in 2022 . That’s good for a  .897 winning 
percentage in those games, ranking No . 9 in the nation .
• In the last two seasons, JMU has averaged 43 .7 points per game at home and owns a margin of victory of 29 .2 
points in those games .
• James Madison has sold out 10 straight regular-season games at Bridgeforth Stadium, as well as 20 of its last 
22 regular-season games at home .
AMONG THE NATION’S BEST
• Once again this week, JMU boasts a top-25 scoring offense and defense . The Dukes have scored 41 .4 points 
per game, good for No . 5 nationally, while allowing just 18 .3 per game (No . 25) .
• The two units continue to play complementary football, as shown in JMU’s +6 turnover margin, which ranks 
No . 19 in the country .
• Offensively, the Dukes appear in the nation’s top 10 in other categories . JMU is No . 1 nationally on fourth downs, 
converting 84 .6%, and No . 10 in red zone scoring ( .951) .
• The Dukes are No . 4 in the country on kickoff returns (35 .43) and No . 11 on punt returns (18 .07) .
• QB Camden Coleman enters Thursday’s game with 14 passing TDs, good for No . 24 in the country and No . 2 
in the Sun Belt .
• CB DJ Kelly has four INTs, which is tied for No . 3 nationally . He also is No . 10 in passes defended (1 .25) .
• WR Michael Scott continues to lead the nation in combined return yardage (709) and is No . 17 in all-purpose 
yards (129 .63) .
• RB George Pettaway leads the Sun Belt in rushing yards (824) and rush yards per carry (6 .65) . He’s second in 
the league in rushing TDs, with seven .
• K Morgan Suarez is No . 14 nationally and leads the conference in scoring, averaging 9 .9 points per game .

QUICK HITS
TALE OF THE TAPE
BREAKING DOWN THE SERIES
• Thursday marks the fifth all-time meeting between 
JMU and Troy .
• The Dukes are 3-1 against the Trojans, including 2-0 
at home .  
• Last season, JMU earned a 31-14 win over Troy in 
the Sun Belt Championship Game at Bridgeforth 
Stadium .
• In the Sun Belt era, the Dukes are 2-0 against the 
Trojans .
• Prior to that, JMU and Troy met twice in the FCS/I-AA 
Playoffs (1994, 1999) .
• The game is sponsored by United Bank and the  
2026 JMU football season is presented by CarMax .

Head Coach Billy Napier
2026 James Madison Football
Billy Napier was hired as James Madison’s 10th head football 
coach on Dec . 6, 2025, and enters his first season at the helm 
in Harrisonburg .
Napier, a 2026 Paul “Bear” Bryant National Coach of the Year 
Watch List selection, welcomed 34 letterwinners back from 
2025’s Sun Belt championship and CFP team and has added 
61 total newcomers, including 39 transfers, to the mix this fall .
Napier boasts a 69-36 career record as a head coach and most 
recently led Florida of the Southeastern Conference for the last 
four years, highlighted by an 8-5 record in 2024 and a win in the 
Gasparilla Bowl over Tulane .
That team won its last four games, including back-to-back top-
25 triumphs over No . 22 LSU and No . 9 Ole Miss, followed by 
topping Florida State on the road and the bowl win over Tulane . 
Other ranked wins included 2025 over No . 9 Texas, 2023 over 
No . 11 Tennessee and 2022 in his Florida debut over No . 7 Utah .
Prior to Florida, Napier authored a dominant run at Louisiana, 
going 40-12 in four seasons from 2018-2021 with four appear -
ances in the Sun Belt Conference Championship game as West 
Division Champions and two league titles in his last two sea -
sons in 2020 and 2021 .
In his first season as a college head coach in 2018, the Ragin’ 
Cajuns went 7-7 overall but won the Sun Belt West at 5-3 with 
a tie for first . Louisiana improved in each of Napier’s seasons, 
including 11-3 (7-1 SBC) in 2019, 10-1 (7-1 SBC) and a league title 
in 2020 and 12-1 (8-0) and a title in 2021 .
The 2019 and 2020 squads won appearances over Miami 
(Ohio) in the LendingTree Bowl and over UTSA in the First Re -
sponder Bowl, respectively  . The 2021 team went on to defeat 
Marshall in the New Orleans Bowl following Napier’s departure 
for Florida . The Ragin’ Cajuns were ranked as high as 15th in the AP 
Poll in 2020 and 16th in 2021 .
Widely touted for his recruiting and player development, each 
of Napier’s recruiting classes at Florida were ranked top 20 in 
the nation . Meanwhile, he had 14 NFL Draft selections in three 
years of drafts at Florida and another seven picks in four years 
at Louisiana . Most recently, seven Gators were taken in the 
2025 NFL Draft . In 2024, wide receiver Ricky Pearsall was a 
first round pick by the 49ers, which followed a first-round selec-
tion of quarterback Anthony Richardson in 2023 . He coached 
an additional 20 NFL Draft picks as an offensive position coach 
across four FBS assistant jobs . That group featured five first-
round selections, consisting of four wide receivers and a run -
ning back .

Fifteen years of experience as an assistant coach helped pre -
pare Napier for his head coaching roles, with 14 coming at the 
FBS level and all 15 on the offensive side . 
He spent two years as a GA at Clemson before then returning to 
Tommy Bowden’s staff in roles as tight ends and recruiting co-
ordinator coach, then quarterbacks and offensive coordinator 
when Dabo Swinney took the helm .
In 2011, Napier joined Nick Saban’s staff at Alabama as an of -
fensive analyst, then followed Jim McElwain from Alabama to 
Colorado State to serve a season as assistant head coach and 
quarterbacks coach . 
He returned to Alabama as wide receiver coach for four sea -
sons (2013-2016) and then spent one year as offensive coordi-
nator and quarterbacks coach at Arizona State in 2017 prior to 
earning the Louisiana job .
Napier was a four-year contributor at Furman (FCS/then I-AA) 
and was a two-time All-Conference quarterback in the South -
ern Conference . 
He led the Paladins to two conference championships and, in 
2001, led Furman to a national runner-up finish in I-AA . 
He set a Furman career record by completing 64 .8% of his 
passes . As the team captain during a record-setting senior 
season, Napier compiled a Furman-record 2,475 passing yards 
and was a finalist for the Walter Payton Award as the nation’s 
most outstanding offensive player .
Napier grew up in Chatsworth, Georgia, and graduated from 
Murray County High School in 1998 . He is married to the for -
mer Ali Gunn, and the couple has a daughter, Annie, and sons, 
Sammy and Charlie . He earned his bachelor’s degree in health 
& exercise science from Furman in 2002 .

PAGE 9

Game Notes
2026 James Madison Football
IN THE POLLS
• This week, JMU earned one point in the US LBM 
Coaches Poll . 
• With the inclusion, JMU has now received votes 
in eight of nine editions of the poll . 
• The Dukes also have received votes in eight of 
nine AP Polls this season .
• The Dukes are the only Sun Belt team to earn 
votes in the AP Poll this season .
• JMU finished last season ranked No . 19 in the AP 
and No . 20 in the Coaches Poll - the first time it 
ever finished a season ranked in the FBS .
• Altogether, JMU has been ranked in 14 AP Polls 
since 2022, received votes in an additional 
28 and have appeared in the poll in all five FBS 
seasons (ranked in 2022, 2023 and 2025) . 
AN UNFORGETTABLE SEASON
• JMU’s inclusion in last season’s College Football 
Playoff was historic for a variety of reasons, 
perhaps most notably being the first Sun Belt 
Conference team to ever make the CFP field .
• The Dukes also were the first team from Virginia 
to compete for a CFP national championship .
• JMU punched its ticket to the CFP in just 
year four playing a Football Bowl Subdivision 
schedule and its second as a full-fledged FBS 
eligible program .
• The 2025 squad won 11 straight games at one 
point, capturing its first Sun Belt Championship .
• James Madison earned the No . 12 seed in last 
year’s CFP, thanks to being one of the five highest-
ranked conference champions .
2025 SUN BELT CHAMPIONS
• JMU used a pair of late touchdowns to pull away 
from Troy for a 31-14 victory to capture the 2025 
Sun Belt Football Championship .
• It marked JMU’s first conference crown at the 
FBS level, the 11th in program history and its first 
since 2021, when JMU closed its chapter in the 
Football Championship Subdivision .
• The win moved JMU to the 12-win mark for the 
first time since that 2021 season .
• JMU became the second straight East Division 
team to win the Sun Belt title and the fourth 
to claim the trophy in the seven overall SBC 
championship games .
• RB Wayne Knight was named the championship’s 
MVP after rushing for a career-high 212 yards 
and setting an SBC title game record with 234 
all-purpose yards .
• DL Sahir West also set title records with career 
bests of 5 .5 tackles for loss and 3 .0 sacks to 
lead the defense .
JMU’S CHAMPIONSHIP HISTORY
• JMU has claimed 11 conference titles in its 
history, with nine as a member of the Colonial 
Athletic Association (formerly Atlantic 10) .
• The Dukes’ first championship came in 1975 as a 
Division III squad in the Virginia College Athletic 
Association .
• JMU won six CAA championships over its last 
seven FCS seasons, winning three straight 
between 2015-17 and 2019-21 . The Dukes won 
their first CAA championship in 1999 and later 
added titles in its 2004 national championship 
season and in 2008 .
• JMU’s first Sun Belt title also was its first won 
in a championship game format, as all previous 
titles were awarded based solely on regular-
season finishes ahead of the FCS Playoffs .
DEFENDING THE CROWN
• The Dukes were voted to finish atop the East 
Division in the 2026 Sun Belt Preseason 
Coaches Poll, which was released on July 13 .
• JMU received 11 of 14 first-place votes and 94 
total points in the poll .
• Following JMU in the East were Old Dominion 
(73 points, 1 first-place vote), Marshall (67), 
Georgia Southern (53, 1 first-place vote), Coastal 
Carolina (45, 1 first-place vote), App State (43) 
and Georgia State (17) .
• In the West Division, Troy, which claimed 12 first-
place votes and 96 points, garnered the top spot .
SUN BELT SUCCESS
• James Madison has earned instant success 
inside the Sun Belt Conference since joining the 
league in 2022 .
• Overall, JMU is 29-8 in conference games in 
that span, including 15-3 at home and 14-5 on 
the road . It also defeated Troy in its first-ever 
Sun Belt Championship Game last season at 
Bridgeforth Stadium .
• The Dukes posted a 12-game conference win 
streak for a span during the 2025 and ‘26 
seasons . They won all eight Sun Belt games last 
year and the first four this year .
• JMU was the only G6 program not to lose a game 
to another G6 in 2025, going 11-0 .
• Against all G6 opponents since joining the Sun 
Belt in 2022, JMU has gone 39-9 ( .813) .
SUN BELT CONT.
• Against the East, JMU is 22-7, which includes 
a 5-0 mark against Georgia State and Old 
Dominion and 4-0 vs . Coastal Carolina .
• JMU holds a 7-1 record against Sun Belt West 
Division squads in league play, as ULM is the only 
team to best JMU, earning a 21-19 win in 2024 .
• Last year, JMU became one of three teams 
to finish a Sun Belt season at 8-0 in the 
championship-game era .
• It was the first time since 2021 that a team 
finished 8-0 in SBC play (Louisiana, coached by 
Billy Napier) .
STACKING WINS
• Since joining the FBS ranks in 2022, JMU has 
won 47 of 59 games ( .797) .
• That is tied for No . 8 for the most wins in college 
football during that time (No . 1 among G6 
programs) with the fifth-best win percentage .
• It’s also the most wins by any SBC team, as Troy 
has 38 since the start of the 2022 season, while 
Marshall has 34 .
• On the road, JMU is 20-8, which are the most 
wins away from home in the country and No . 5 in 
road win percentage ( .714) .
SUCCESS SPREADS
• Including this season, JMU has now recorded 13 
straight winning seasons, dating back to 2014 .
• The squad also has produced at least a  .500 
record or better in 24 straight seasons .
• What’s more, the Dukes have been led by six 
different head coaches in that span of success .
• James Madison last was under  .500 in 2002 (5-
7), one of two seasons this millennium with more 
losses than victories . 
• The Dukes have turned in eight 10-win seasons 
since its first national championship campaign 
in 2004 .
• Dating back to year one of the program in 1972, 
JMU has a winning record in 37 of 55 seasons .
JMU’S SUCCESS AT HOME
• Since 2016, visitors have not seen much success 
against the Dukes inside Bridgeforth Stadium .
• In that time, JMU has turned in a 69-5 record 
( .932) at home, which has included five unbeaten 
regular seasons in Harrisonburg .
• That also includes winning all 33 non-conference 
home games, 11 of which occurred in the FCS 
playoffs between 2016-21 .
• Since joining the FBS, JMU is 26-3 ( .897) at home .
• JMU has produced a pair of 19-game home win 
streaks this decade (2016-18 and 2018-21) .
• During its FCS powerhouse days, JMU went 9-0 
at home twice, holding homefield advantage to 
the FCS title game in both 2017 and 2019 .
• Since the 2016 national title campaign, JMU has 
never finished worse than 5-1 at home .
• The last non-conference opponent to defeat 
JMU in Harrisonburg was Colgate on Dec . 5, 
2015, in the second round of the FCS Playoffs .

Additional Notes
2026 James Madison Football
PRESEASON ALL-SUN BELT
• Along with being named the East favorite in the 
Sun Belt’s Preseason Coaches Poll, three Dukes 
also earned Preseason All-Sun Belt honors .
• OL Zach Greenberg and EDGE Amar Thomas 
were named to the league’s preseason first team 
while RB George Pettaway claimed second-team 
laurels .
• Greenberg was a Second Team All-Sun Belt 
selection in 2025, while Thomas was placed on 
the Honorable Mention list to close last season .
• For Pettaway, the preseason nod has now come 
in consecutive seasons .
• The preseason all-conference team was 
selected by the 14 head coaches and one media 
member from each institution .
HERO ALL-AMERICANS
• A pair of Dukes entered the 2026 season with 
preseason All-America accolades .
• EDGE Amar Thomas and OL Zach Greenberg 
were named to the 2026 HERO Sports 
Preseason Group of Six All-American Team . 
Thomas was placed on the first team, while 
Greenberg garnered second-team recognition .
• JMU saw four players end the 2025 campaign 
with G5 All-America honors (First: Trent 
Hendrick, Wayne Knight, Pat McMurtrie; Third: 
Alonza Barnett III) .
RACKING UP RECOGNITION
• OL Zach Greenberg received plenty of preseason 
praise entering his seventh season of college 
football .
• Along with being named to the Preseason All-
Sun Belt Team and a HERO Sports Preseason 
Group of Six All-American, the Livingston, N .J ., 
native also appeared on the Lombardi Award and 
Outland Trophy watch lists .
• He also was picked for the Lombardi Award 
Midseason Watch List on Oct . 14 .
• Led by the Rotary Club of Houston, the Lombardi 
Award is given annually to the top lineman or 
linebacker in college football .
• The Outland Trophy is headed by the Football 
Writers Association of America (FWAA), which 
awards the nation’s best interior lineman .
CAMPBELL AWARD SEMIFINALIST
• On Sept . 23, Greenberg was named one of 206 
semifinalists for the 2026 William V  . Campbell 
Trophy, which honors college football’s premier 
scholar-athlete .
• Now in its 37th year, the Campbell Award 
recognizes an individual as the absolute 
best football scholar-athlete in the nation 
for his combined academic success, football 
performance and exemplary leadership .
• Greenberg graduated with a bachelor’s degree 
in computer science and is currently pursuing 
a master’s in adult education/human resource 
development .
SCOTT ON JET AWARD WATCH LIST 
• On Oct . 14, WR Michael Scott was named to the 
Jet Award Midseason Watch List, which honors 
the most outstanding return man in college 
football .
• Scott is the nation’s leader in combined return 
yardage, with 709 .
• He also is No . 1 nationally in kick returns (38 .8) 
and No . 2 in punt returns (18 .8) .
• Scott is one of five players in the country with 
two punt return TDs this season - he did it in the 
same game (Sept . 12 vs . Wagner) .
GP HITS 100
• RB George Pettaway reached the century mark 
on the ground in four straight games from Sept . 
26-Oct . 17 .
• It’s a feat that hadn’t been accomplished by a 
Duke since Khalid Abdullah in 2016 .
• Pettaway ran 16 times for 110 yards at Old 
Dominion, 14 for a career-high 206 and two TDs 
vs . Marshall, 21 for 151 at Georgia Southern and 
14 for 119 and a score vs . Georgia State .
• Through eight games, he leads the Sun Belt in 
rushing (824) by nearly 250 yards .
• He’s also one yard away from rushing for 2,000 
in his JMU career .
FLOWE GOODWORKS
• S KJ Flowe’s community impact was recognized 
in a big way this preseason .
• Flowe was named an Allstate AFCA Good Works 
Team nominee and the Wuerffel Trophy Watch 
List, which honors players who lead through 
community service while excelling in the 
classroom and on the field .
• Flowe has made a profound impact on the 
youth of Harrisonburg and the surrounding area 
through his community service endeavors and 
other philanthropic activities .
• He is the fourth Duke to be tabbed to the list, 
joining Camden Wise (2023), Jacob Dobbs 
(2024), and Blake Kendall (2025) .
BACK FOR 2026
• A total of 48 players have returned this season 
following JMU’s historic 2025 campaign .
• In all, the Dukes feature 34 returning 
letterwinners, including five starters .
• On offense, OL Zach Greenberg started all 14 
games for James Madison last season, while 
EDGE Amar Thomas and LB Gannon Weathersby 
and logged 14 and 11 starts at JMU in 2025, 
respectively .
• K Morgan Suarez and P Patrick Rea again head 
up the squad’s special teams unit .
• Additionally, five other Dukes on the 2026 roster 
have made at least one start in the purple and 
gold prior to this season: RB George Pettaway 
(11), WR Dylan Williams (2), WR Michael Scott (1), 
LB Jaylen Studio (1), WR Braeden Wisloski (1) .
PUNTING ... AND THROWING FOR A CAUSE
• This season, P Patrick Rea is supporting the 
Robert Connor Dawes (RCD) Foundation by 
donating $100 for every punt downed inside the 
opponents’ 20-yard line .
• The cause is close to Rea’s heart, who lost his 
sister, Pippa, to childhood brain cancer in 2015 .
• The same illness took John Paul Macri, a young 
JMU fan who became close to the program over 
the past two seasons, on Oct . 4 .
• The RCA Foundation supports families affected 
by childhood brain cancer and funds critical 
research in the U .S ., as well as Rea’s home 
country of Australia .
• After eight games, Rea has placed 12 punts 
inside the 20 to help his cause .
• QB Camden Coleman joined in Rea’s effort 
midseason and pledged $100 to RCD for each 
TD pass he throws this season .
JMU IN LEAGUE OPENERS
• JMU is 25-9 in league openers following its 
2026 Sun Belt lidlifting victory at Old Dominion 
on Sept . 26 .
• The Dukes have won 11 of their last 12 conference 
openers .
• The lone setback in that time came in 2024 at 
ULM, which snapped a nine-game conference-
opening win streak that spanned from 2015-23 
Coach Billy Napier
-Paul “Bear” Bryant National Coach of the Year

TEN STRAIGHT FOR JMU NATION
• Following the Georgia State game, James 
Madison has now sold out 10 straight regular-
season, home games .
• Last season, James Madison sold out the regular 
season, going six-for-six at Bridgeforth Stadium for 
the first time since the venue’s expansion in 2011 .
• Additionally, all four home games this season have 
also been sold out .
• Overall, JMU has sold out 20 of its last 22 regular-
season home games with five sellouts each in 
2023 and 2024 .
• JMU averaged 25,135 spectators per game at 
home last season .
BRIDGEFORTH TOP ATTENDANCES
• In 2023, JMU set a single-season attendance 
record with 152,234 fans watching the Dukes at 
Bridgeforth Stadium .
• That was an average of 25,372 spectators per 
game, which is 102% of the stadium’s capacity .
• On Aug . 21, JMU sold out season tickets for the 
fourth consecutive season, proving the Dukes’ 
faithful are ready for more football .
• Below are JMU’s top-10 home attendances .

RECORDS WHEN IN ‘26...JMU ACCEPTS MEMBERSHIP TO AMERICAN
• On Oct . 12, JMU was accepted for admission into the 
American Conference following university application 
and a unanimous vote of the American CEOs .
• The Dukes will join as a full league member no later 
than July 1, 2028 .
• With the move, 17 of JMU’s 18 sports will fall under 
one conference .
• Currently, the American features 13 “full” members, 
as well as Army and Navy in football only .
KILLER CAM
• Following his four-touchdown night at Old 
Dominion, QB Camden Coleman was named the 
Sun Belt Offensive Player of the Week on Sept . 28 .
• Coleman completed his first nine passes of the 
night and 14 of his first 15 .
• He finished 24/42 for 243 yards and the scores, 
while also adding 17 rushing yards .
• All four of his touchdown passes went to different 
receivers .
• The weekly honor was the third of Coleman’s 
career but his first at JMU . He was twice named 
the CAA Offensive Player of the Week during his 
time at Richmond (2023-24) .
REA FLIPS FIELD; HONORED BY SUN BELT
• On Sept . 21, Rea was named the Sun Belt 
Special Teams Player of the Week following his 
performance at San Diego State (W 26-13) .
• Rea played a key role in starting field position, 
placing three punts inside the 20 and a fourth on 
the 20 .
• In all, he punted six times for 248 yards (41 .3 avg) .
• It was the second straight week a Duke won the 
conference honor - WR Michael Scott (Week 2) .
• He became the first JMU punter to win the award 
since Ryan Hanson on Oct . 23, 2023 .
A RECORD-SETTING DAY
• On Sept . 12, James Madison enjoyed a historic 
day in an 87-3 win over Wagner at Bridgeforth 
Stadium .
• It marked the most points scored in single-game 
program history and the most by any FBS team 
since South Alabama topped Northwestern State, 
87-3, in 2024 .
• Of note, no FBS team has eclipsed 87 points in a 
game since 1991 . 
• Additionally, JMU’s 84-point margin of victory set 
a new program record and is the largest in FBS 
college football since 2012, when Oklahoma State 
defeated Savannah State, 84-0 .
• JMU returned three punts for touchdowns in the 
win, tying for the single-game NCAA record, which 
was last achieved by USC in its 2013 game against 
Cal .
• The Dukes tied the program record with 12 
touchdowns on the day - six rushing, three 
passing, three special teams .
• Individually, WR Michael Scott totaled 303 all-
purpose yards, good for fourth-most in JMU 
history . He became one of three Dukes to return 
two punts for touchdowns in one game and was 
named the Sun Belt Special Teams Player of the 
Week for his efforts . 
GREAT SCOTT!
• Wide receiver Michael Scott turned in one of the 
most impressive days in the return game in JMU 
history on Sept . 12, vs . Wagner .
• Scott finished with 303 all-purpose yards in the 
87-3 win, including two punt return touchdowns 
and a 92-yard kick return .
• He scored on a 76-yard punt return in the second 
quarter and took his second 69 yards for a TD .
• Scott became one of three Dukes in program 
history to return two punts for touchdowns in one 
game .
• His 303 all-purpose yards rank No . 4 in single-
game program history .
• With the effort, Scott was named the Sun Belt 
Special Teams Player of the Week on Sept . 14 .
FIRST GAME, FIRST WIN
• JMU coach Billy Napier’s debut with the Dukes 
was a successful one, as James Madison earned 
a 20-13 win over Liberty on Sept . 5 .
• With the win, Napier improved to 5-4 in his career 
in season openers .
• He also became the fifth JMU coach to win their 
first game with the Dukes, joining Joe Purzycki 
(1985), Alex Wood (1995), Mike Houston (2016) 
and Bob Chesney (2024) .
• In all, the 10 head coaches at JMU have combined 
to go 5-5 in their first game at the helm .

NFL DUKES
• As of Oct . 25, six former JMU Dukes are on NFL 
rosters .
• That list includes CB Elijah Culp (Green Bay - 
PS), WR Nick DeGennaro (Washington - PS), LB 
Xavier Holmes (New England - PS), DE Jamree 
Kromah (Chicago), OL Aaron Stinnie (N .Y . Giants) 
and S Jacob Thomas (Minnesota) .
KINGS OF THE COMMONWEALTH
• Following Week 4’s victory at Old Dominion, 
JMU has won 24 straight games against 
Commonwealth foes .
• Last season’s triumph at Liberty marked the 
100th all-time victory against a state opponent 
since the start of the program in 1972 .
• Overall, JMU is 103-61 ( .628) against Virginia squads .
• JMU also is 9-0 over Virginia teams since 
moving up to the FBS, including 2-0 this season .
• The Dukes opened the 2026 campaign with a 
win over in-state foe Liberty .
• William & Mary was the last in-state team to 
beat JMU in a 44-41 decision on Oct . 31, 2015, 
in Williamsburg .
FAST STARTS
• JMU’s offense has wasted no time getting going 
so far this season .
• The squad has scored touchdowns on six of 
its eight opening offensive possessions this 
season .
• JMU opened the season with a 12-play, 80-
yard drive vs . Liberty . Then, it recorded a 3-play, 
5-yard drive vs . Wagner . At San Diego State, the 
Dukes used a 6-play, 75-yard scoring drive, while 
the ODU game saw the team go 7 plays for 53 
yards, all ending in trips to the end zone .
• The Dukes restarted the streak with an 8-play, 
75-yard TD drive vs . Georgia State, before doing 
it again at App State .
STRONG AFTER OPEN WEEKS
• Since the 2014 season, JMU has turned in solid 
results coming off an open week, posting a 12-1 
record after not having played the week prior .
• Over its last four games coming off a bye, JMU 
has won each by at least three scores .
• In addition to the 31-13 victory at Liberty last 
season, JMU handled North Carolina (70-50) 
and Georgia State (38-7) in 2024, and Georgia 
Southern (41-13) in 2023 .
• In JMU’s first FBS season, an early open weekend 
gave way to its thrilling 32-28 comeback win at 
App State in 2022, which was JMU’s first Sun 
Belt game ever .
• While a member of the FCS and Colonial Athletic 
Association, JMU was a perfect 7-0 following a 
bye week between 2014 and 2021 (excluding the 
2020 spring COVID season) .
BEST RECORD IN A TRANSITION
• JMU completed its reclassification from FCS to 
FBS with the best record in history .
• Over two seasons (2022-23), JMU put together 
a 19-5 overall record while playing full FBS 
schedules .
• That also included finishing atop the Sun Belt 
East Division in each of those seasons, going a 
combined 13-3 in conference bouts .
• In 2023, JMU finished with an 11-2 mark after 
facing Air Force in the Lockheed Martin Armed 
Forces Bowl .
• It was JMU’s first Bowl Season berth, and despite 
going through reclassification and due to a lack 
of bowl-eligible teams, JMU earned a bid .
• The Dukes won 10 games over FBS opponents 
in 2023, which is a record for any year-two 
transitioning team .
• In 2022, JMU started 5-0 and became the 
inaugural first-year transition team to earn an 
AP Top 25 ranking .
• The Dukes were ranked in the AP Top 25 for seven 
straight polls entering the 2023 postseason and 
in the US LBM Coaches Poll for six of seven .

Game 8: App State 34, James Madison 31 (OT)

 James Madison 7 7 7 10 0 31
 App State 0 10 3 18 3 34
Oct . 22, 2026 | Kidd Brewer Stadium | Boone, N .C . | Attendance: 33,322
Dukes Fall to App State in Overtime
BOONE, N.C. – James Madison suffered its first loss of the 2026 campaign on 
Thursday night at App State, falling 34-31 in overtime .
The Dukes (7-1, 4-1 Sun Belt) tied the game on a 6-yard touchdown run by quar -
terback Camden Coleman with three seconds left in regulation but allowed an App 
State game-winning field goal in the extra frame in front of a sold-out crowd at Kidd 
Brewer Stadium .
JMU outgained the Mountaineers, 423-366, but was outscored, 24-17, after half -
time .
The Dukes took the ball and scored on their first offensive possession for the sixth 
time in eight games, going eight plays for 75 yards in 3:27 . JMU marched down the 
field and converted on a fourth-down in the red zone, leading to a 1-yard plunge into 
the end zone by running back George Pettaway .
Those would be the only points of the opening frame . JMU’s defense forced the 
Mountaineers into punts on their first four possession of the night, before the hosts 
got on the board with a 48-yard field goal early in the second quarter .
JMU hit a big play at the 4:48 mark before halftime to extend its lead . Quarterback JC 
Evans connected with wide receiver Braeden Wisloski for a 70-yard score to make 
it 14-3 .
But App State (5-2, 2-1 Sun Belt) answered at the end of the half, producing a 10-
play, 75-yard drive that was finished with Alexander Sanchez’s 19-yard touchdown 
reception .
Still, JMU led, 14-10, at halftime .
The Mountaineers cut their deficit to just one point with a field goal to open the sec -
ond half . That led to another JMU touchdown drive to bump the lead back to 21-13 
with 2:38 to play in the third, as Coleman found tight end Cole Keller for a 19-yard 
score .
In the fourth, App State tied the game with 13:15 to play on Chris Lofton’s 5-yard 
touchdown reception and a successful two-point conversion . 
Then, kicker Morgan Suarez helped the Dukes retake the lead with a 47-yard field goal 
with 9:19 to go . It matched his career high and put the visitors up, 24-21 .
From there, the Mountaineers scored 10 straight points to take a 31-24 lead with 5:52 
to go . First, App State tied the game on a 36-yard field goal, before it took the lead 
with a short touchdown following a JMU interception .
But the Dukes orchestrated a late rally, tying the game in the closing seconds . Cole-
man’s score capped a clutch, 15-play, 71-yard drive to keep JMU alive . 
However, JMU hit the upright on a field goal attempt in overtime, moments before the Mountaineers ended it with James London’s 40-yarder .
Coleman finished 22-of-39 for 232 yards and a touchdown through the air, to go along with 11 yards and a score with his legs . Evans added 99 passing yards and a 
touchdown .
Wide receiver Jeremiah Harrison led the way in the receiving game with six catches for 59 yards, while Wisloski caught two passes for 84 yards and the score .
Linebackers Nakian Jackson and JT Hooten and safety Seth Gathers each had nine tackles to lead the defense . Edge Turner Schmidt added two sacks .
Punter Patrick Rea punted seven times for a long of 56 . In all, he booted two of 50-plus yards and placed two inside the App State 20-yard line . 
Following Thursday’s game, the Dukes fell to 6-15 against App State .
Up next, JMU returns home to take on Troy on Thursday, Oct . 29, at Bridgeforth Stadium . Kickoff is set for 7:30 p .m . ET, on ESPN2 . 
GAME NOTES
• App State is JMU’s sixth-most played team in program history (21 meetings) . 
• JMU is now 29-8 against Sun Belt foes since entering the league in 2022, as well as 22-7 vs . the East Division .
• In Sun Belt road games, the Dukes are now 14-5 .
• Thursday’s game was JMU’s first overtime contest since Nov . 30, 2024, against Marshall .
• JMU and App State also played an overtime game in Harrisonburg in 2023 .
• JMU scored a touchdown on its first offensive possession for the sixth time in eight games this season .
• JMU’s defense forced punts on App State’s first four possessions of the night .
• The Dukes’ 15-play touchdown drive in the fourth quarter tied its longest of the season .
• JMU held App State to just 81 rushing yards on 30 attempts (2 .6 average) .
•James Madison attempted 46 passes in the game, the most this season .

Rushing Leader: Jaquari Lewis (App): 13 carries, 74 yards
Receiving Leader: Braeden Wisloski (JMU): 2 catches, 84 yards, TD
Tackles Leader: Colton Phares (App): 13 tackles, TFL, INT

2026 James Madison Football
Game 7: James Madison 49, Georgia State 22
    1 2 3 4 Score 
 Georgia State  7 3 6 6 22
 James Madison  14 21 7 7 49
Oct . 17, 2026 | Bridgeforth Stadium | Harrisonburg, Va . | Attendance: 25,225
Dukes Dominate in Georgia State Win
HARRISONBURG, Va. – James Madison tallied 515 yards of total offense and went a per-
fect 6-for-6 in the red zone in a 49-22 win over Georgia State on Saturday afternoon at 
Bridgeforth Stadium .
The Dukes remained perfect in the win, improving to 7-0 for the fifth time in program his -
tory and first since 2023 . In front of a Homecoming crowd of 25,225, JMU earned its 12th 
straight regular-season Sun Belt victory and its 11th win in a row at home .
JMU’s quarterback duo of Camden Coleman and JC Evans shined in the win, as the two 
combined for three passing and two rushing touchdowns . 
The Dukes (7-0, 4-0 Sun Belt) opened the game with an 8-play, 75-yard touchdown drive 
to take the early lead . A 42-yard catch by wide receiver Jeremiah Harrison set up the 
score, which was cashed in by Evans on a 2-yard touchdown run .
But GSU (4-2, 1-1 Sun Belt) answered on its first possession, registering a 65-yard touch-
down drive capped by a 1-run plunge into the end zone to tie the game .
From there, wide receiver Michael Scott showed off his speed . First, he returned a kickoff 76 
yards to put JMU into scoring position, before he caught a 20-yard touchdown pass from 
Coleman to make it 14-7 .
Scott finished with four catches for 44 yards and the score, and he also tallied a 32-yard 
rush in the win . 
In the second quarter, Evans connected with tight end Cole Keller for a 41 catch and run, 
before Evans tallied his second rushing score of the day from 1-yard out . That made it a 21-7 
game with 13:59 to play before halftime .
Then, the Dukes successfully executed an onside kick to grab an extra possession and 
made the most of it . Coleman found tight end KD Mosely for a 19-yard touchdown to extend 
the lead to three scores .
Following a GSU field goal, JMU put up one more first-half scoring drive . Wide receiver Co -
rey Scott caught a 44-yard pass from Coleman, which led to running back Seth Cromwell’s 
3-yard touchdown run .
The Dukes put up 303 yards of total offense and led, 35-10, at halftime .
GSU began the scoring half with a 32-yard touchdown catch from Rodshaun Dorsey to cut 
JMU’s lead to 35-16 after a two-point conversion failed .
Running back George Pettaway scored his seventh rushing touchdown of the year at the 
7:27 mark of the third quarter . He found the open field on a 12-yard score to make it 42-16 . 
That came moments after the Duke defense forced a Panther turnover on downs .
Pettaway finished with 14 carries for 119 yards and the score, his fourth straight 100-yard rushing performance .
In the fourth quarter, the two teams traded scores as GSU reached the end zone with 7:10 to play, before the Dukes capped the day with a 22-yard touchdown reception from wide 
receiver Miles Halbert .
In all, nine different receivers caught a pass for JMU on Saturday .
Coleman finished 13-of-19 for 185 yards and two touchdowns through the air, as well as 26 yards on four carries on the ground . Meanwhile, Evans went 4-of-8 for 80 yards and a 
passing touchdown to go along with six carries, 21 yards and two rushing scores . 
Defensively, the Dukes were led by linebacker JT Hooten’s 10 tackles, while linebacker Nakian Jackson had seven, with a sack . Edge Ernest Willor Jr . had two sacks in the win, while 
cornerback DJ Kelly collected his team-leading fourth interception of the year and cornerback Roddrey McWilliams had a late fumble recovery .
JMU limited the Panthers to just 185 yards passing on the day .
With the win, the Dukes improved to 6-0 against Georgia State all time, including 4-0 at home . Saturday marked JMU’s 10th straight regular-season sellout at Bridgeforth Stadium, 
as well as its 20th in the last 22 games .
Up next, JMU endures a quick turnaround, as the Dukes travel to App State for matchup on Thursday, Oct . 22 . Kickoff in Boone, North Carolina, is set for 7 p .m . ET, and the game will 
air live on ESPNU .
GAME NOTES
• JMU improved to 29-7 against Sun Belt foes since entering the league, as well as 22-6 vs . the East Division .
• The Dukes are now 15-3 in Sun Belt play at home .
• JMU finished with 515 yards of total offense, including 303 in the first half . The Dukes are now 15-2 in the FBS era when totaling at least 500 yards in a game .
• The Dukes scored 21 points in the second quarter, the sixth time they have tallied 20 or more in one frame this season .
• The defense also has forced multiple turnovers in six straight games .
• The Dukes finished a perfect 6-for-6 in the red zone with all six ending in touchdowns .
• JMU scored a touchdown on its first offensive possession for the fifth time in seven games this season .
• Saturday also marked JMU’s 10th straight regular-season sellout at Bridgeforth Stadium, as well as its 20th in the last 22 regular-season home games .

Game 6: James Madison 27, Georgia Southern 24
    1 2 3 4 Score

 Georgia Southern  7 3 0 14 24
Oct . 10, 2026 | Allen E . Paulson Stadium | Statesboro, Ga . | Attendance: 20,545
Dukes Grind Out Road Win at Georgia Southern
STATESBORO, Ga. – James Madison overcame a four-point halftime deficit to take 
down Georgia Southern, 27-24, on Saturday night at Allen . E . Paulson Stadium .
The Dukes (6-0, 3-0 Sun Belt) scored 17 unanswered points at one point, helping 
them earn their first win in Statesboro in program history . The win moves the squad 
to 6-0 for just the fifth time ever and first since 2023 . 
Georgia Southern (2-4, 1-1 Sun Belt) took the lead early by opening the game with a 
5-play, 75-yard touchdown drive . JMU, meanwhile, started slow offensively, punting 
and turning the ball over on downs on its first two possessions .
Then, the Dukes capitalized off an interception by cornerback Ken Willis, getting on 
the board with a 33-yard field goal by kicker Morgan Suarez with 54 seconds to play 
in the first quarter .
It stayed that way until the Eagles made a field goal of their own to make it 10-3 with 
3:25 to play before halftime . From there, Suarez made his second field goal of the 
night from 21 yards as the first half expired .
Georgia Southern led, 10-6, at the break . 
The Dukes grabbed their first lead of the night with a scoring drive to open the second 
half . A 29-yard catch by wide receiver Michael Scott and 35-yard run by running back 
George Pettaway set up wide receiver Jeremiah Harrison’s 2-yard touchdown grab 
from quarterback Camden Coleman to put the visitors up, 13-10 .
JMU got the ball right back when linebacker Nakian Jackson jumped on a fumble, 
which led to a 57-yard touchdown pass from quarterback JC Evans to tight end Cole 
Keller on a key, fourth-and-1 play .
That made it 20-10 at the 7:48 mark of the third .
In need of a spark, Georgia Southern orchestrated an 11-play, 73-yard touchdown 
drive to draw within three of the lead at 20-17 with 12:50 to play in the game . 
But JMU bumped the lead back to 10 at the 5:27 mark when tight end KD Mosley 
scored off a 1-yard pass from Evans . The hosts wouldn’t quit, however, cutting the 
Dukes’ lead to 27-24 with 2:27 to go .
After holding on defense, the Eagles had a chance to tie the game in the closing moments but a 52-yard field goal fell short .
JMU finished with 438 yards of total offense in the win . Pettaway tallied 151 yards on 21 carries, good for his third consecutive 100-yard rushing performance . Keller led 
the way in the receiving game with four catches for 108 yards and a touchdown .
Coleman threw for 171 yards and a score on 16-of-27 passing, while Evans, who saw his first action since Week 1, went 2-of-2 for 58 yards and two scores . He added 15 
yards on seven carries on the ground .
Defensive back Jayon Harvey led the way defensively, securing nine tackles, including eight solos .
With the win, JMU improved to 4-9 all time against Georgia Southern, including 1-6 on the road . It also marked the squad’s 11th straight regular-season Sun Belt victory . 
JMU is now bowl eligible for the fourth consecutive season since completing its transition to the FBS . 
Up next, the Dukes return home to take on Georgia State in a Homecoming matchup on Saturday, Oct .17 . Kickoff at Bridgeforth Stadium is set for noon ET, and the game 
will air live on ESPNU .
GAME NOTES
• Saturday marked the 13th all-time meeting between James Madison and Georgia Southern .
• With the win, the Dukes improved to 4-9 against the Eagles, including 1-6 on the road .
• The victory was JMU’s first in Statesboro .
• JMU also improved to 21-6 against Sun Belt East Division foes since entering the league .
• With the win, the Dukes improved to 6-0 for just the fifth time in program history, including the first since 2023 .
• It also was JMU’s 11th straight regular-season Sun Belt win .
• For Coach Billy Napier, the win pushed his Sun Belt regular-season win streak to 16 games, dating back to 2021 with Louisiana .
• Since joining the FBS in 2022, the Dukes have won a nation-leading 20 road games .
• JMU trailed at halftime for the first time this season .
• JMU finished with 438 yards of total offense, including 254 in the second half .
• RB George Pettaway became the first Duke to record three straight 100-yard rushing performances since Khalid Abdullah in 2016
• The Dukes have recorded at least one interception on defense in four straight games .
• The defense also has forced multiple turnovers in five straight games .
• K Morgan Suarez has now made 26 field goals in his JMU career, good for No . 9 in program history

 James Madison  10 28 0 7 45
Oct . 3, 2026 | Bridgeforth Stadium | Harrisonburg, Va . | Attendance: 20,034
Dukes Improve to 5-0 with Marshall Win
HARRISONBURG, Va. –  James Madison scored 28 unanswered points in the sec -
ond quarter en route to a 45-17 win over Marshall on Saturday afternoon at Bridge-
forth Stadium .
The Dukes (5-0, 2-0 Sun Belt) registered 573 yards of total offense, helping them 
to their 10th straight Sun Belt Conference regular-season victory . In front of a sold-
out, Family Weekend crowd of 25,034, JMU earned its first-ever home victory over 
the Thundering Herd (3-2, 0-1 Sun Belt .)
Quarterback Camden Coleman finished 21-of-32 for 263 yards and three touch -
downs in the victory . Wide receiver Corey Scott caught a pair of touchdown passes, 
including a 53-yarder in the first quarter . He logged five total catches for 104 yards . 
On the ground, running back George Pettaway turned in a career night, finishing with 
206 yards and two scores on just 14 carries . Defensively, cornerback DJ Kelly had 
two interceptions .
Marshall started the scoring with a 14-play, 89-yard touchdown drive in the first 
quarter . It marked the first time the Dukes had trailed in a game at any point all sea -
son . 
But JMU needed just three plays on the ensuing possession to even the score at 7  . 
Coleman found Scott for a 53-yard catch and run into the end zone at the 5:40 mark 
of the opening frame .
Then, kicker Morgan Suarez made a 22-yard field goal on the Dukes’ next offensive 
possession, giving JMU a 10-7 lead .
The Herd regained the lead on the second play of the second quarter to cap a 76-yard 
drive to make it 14-10 .
Coleman connected with Scott for the second time in the first half at the 11:52 mark 
of the second quarter for a 12-yard touchdown . It marked the fourth lead change and 
put JMU on top, 17-14 .
Pettaway extended the lead later in the quarter on a 73-yard touchdown run to make 
it 24-14 . It was the longest run of his career and fifth trip to the end zone this season .
Following an interception by Kelly, Pettaway added a second quarter score on a 33-
yard touchdown scamper to give the Dukes a 31-14 lead . The Suffolk, Virginia, native 
became the first Duke to rush for 200 yards in a game since Wayne Knight in the Sun 
Belt Championship Game against Troy on Dec . 5, 2025 . 
Just before the half, wide receiver Noah Grevious caught a 20-yard touchdown to make it 38-14 . JMU scored 28 second-quarter points, the most in any quarter this 
season . 
In all, the Dukes tallied 387 yards of total offense in the first half . 
Marshall scored the first points of the second half with a 27-yard field goal at the 7:18 mark of the third quarter  . The Herd recovered a JMU fumble to get into scoring 
position .
Running back Nick Herman claimed the only other points of the half, capping the scoring with a 3-yard touchdown in the fourth quarter, his third of the year .
The Dukes allowed just three points and 189 yards in the second half . Linebacker JT Hooten led the way with a team-high eight tackles .
With the win, JMU improved to 3-4 all time against Marshall . The squad is now 5-0 for the first time since 2023 .
Up next, the Dukes return to the road to take on Georgia Southern on Saturday, Oct . 10 . Kickoff at Allen E . Paulson Stadium in Statesboro is set for 7 or 7:30 p .m ., on 
ESPN2 or ESPNU . 
GAME NOTES
• Saturday marked the seventh all-time meeting between James Madison and Marshall .
• With the win, the Dukes moved to 3-4 all time against the Thundering Hard .
• It marked JMU’s first-ever win over Marshall at home .
• The Dukes improved to 20-6 against Sun Belt East Division foes since entering the league .
• Overall, JMU is now 25-3 at home since joining the FBS ranks in 2022 .
• The win pushed the Dukes to 5-0 for the fourth time in the last seven seasons and first since 2023 .
• It also was JMU’s 10th straight regular-season Sun Belt win .
• The Dukes scored 28 points in the second quarter, the most of any quarter all season .
• Down 7-0, JMU trailed for the first time in 2026 .
• JMU is now 14-2 in the FBS when it gains at least 500 yards in a game since 2022 .
• JMU also earned its ninth straight regular-season sellout at Bridgeforth Stadium and 19th in its last 21 regular season games .

Sept . 26, 2026 | S .B . Ballard Stadium | Norfolk, Va . | Attendance: 20,035
JMU Opens Sun Belt Play with Win at Old Dominion 
NORFOLK, Va. – James Madison opened Sun Belt Conference play with a 46-20 win over Old 
Dominion in the TowneBank Royal Rivalry on Saturday night at S .B . Ballard Stadium in Norfolk .
JMU (4-0, 1-0 SBC) took the lead early and never relinquished it, piling up 405 yards of total of -
fense . Defensively, the Dukes registered nine sacks, tying the program’s FBS record, and held the 
Monarchs (1-3, 0-1 SBC) to -14 rushing yards on the night .
Quarterback Camden Coleman threw four touchdown passes in the win . He went 24-of-42 for 243 
yards through the air, helping JMU improve to 4-0 for the sixth time in the last seven seasons .
JMU got points on all nine trips to the red zone as five different players found the end zone . 
After JMU was able to get a three-and-out on defense to start the game, the Dukes opened the 
scoring with a 3-yard touchdown catch by tight end Cole Keller . It marked the fourth straight 
game that JMU scored a touchdown on its first offensive possession to begin the season .
The Dukes added on later in the frame when Coleman connected with wide receiver Michael Scott 
for a 20-yard score, making it 14-0 .
In all, Coleman completed his first nine passes of the night and 14 of his first 15 . 
Down a pair of scores, ODU responded with a four-play, 75-yard touchdown drive in just 1:36 to get 
back in the game . Leon Haughton Jr . hauled in a 23-yard touchdown to cut the Dukes’ advantage to 
14-7 with 4:14 to play in the first quarter .
Then, Michael Scott made a big play on the ensuing kickoff, tallying a 72-yard return, which set up 
more JMU points . Kicker Morgan Suarez made a 28-yard attempt to give the Dukes a 17-7 advan -
tage . 
JMU opened the second quarter with more points, as the Dukes orchestrated a 6-play, 60-yard 
touchdown drive . Wide receiver Jeremiah Harrison brought down a 13-yard score from Coleman to 
extend the lead to 23-7 .
From there, the Dukes forced a Monarch turnover as safety TJ McGill pounced on a fumble, caused 
by defensive lineman Mason Purham . Three plays later, Coleman tossed his fourth touchdown pass 
of the first half, an 18-yard connection to wide receiver Corey Scott .
ODU made it a 30-14 game with 4:37 to play before halftime by taking advantage of the Dukes’ lone 
turnover, as Mauki James scored on a 1-yard plunge into the end zone
In the final moments of the first half, the Monarchs made a 49-yard field goal to cut the JMU lead 
to 30-17 at the break .
JMU tallied 255 yards of total offense in the first half and recorded six sacks in the opening 30 
minutes .
Suarez added three second-half field goals to help the Dukes pull away . He opened the third quarter 
with a 36-yarder, before making a kick from 27 yards out later on to make it a 33-17 game . 
The Wellington, Florida, native capped his night with a 26-yard make with 8:54 to go in the game, 
his career-high fourth made field goal of the game . Suarez is now 9-for-9 in field goal attempts to 
begin the 2026 season .
The Dukes found the end zone just once in the second half, a 1-yard touchdown run by running back Seth Cromwell with 2:55 remaining . Still, the visitors outscored ODU, 16-3, after halftime . 
Other top performers included running back George Pettaway, who ran for 110 yards on 16 carries, including a long of 35 yards . Wide receiver Braeden Wisloski led the way in the receiving game 
with a team-high five catches for 49 yards .
Linebacker Nakian Jackson recorded nine tackles to lead the JMU defense, while four different players had multiple sacks on the night . 
Additionally, defensive back Jamal Olford had a fourth-quarter interception, the first of his career . 
With the win, the Dukes improved to 5-2 all time against Old Dominion . Of note, all five wins over the Monarchs have come in the Sun Belt era . JMU has now won 24 straight games against in-state 
competition . 
JMU has now won its conference opener in 11 of the last 12 seasons and is now 25-9 all time in league lidlifters .
Up next, the Dukes return home to take on Marshall on Saturday, Oct . 3 . Kickoff at Bridgeforth Stadium is set for 3:45 p .m ., ET, and the game will be aired live on ESPNU .
GAME NOTES
• Saturday marked the seventh all-time meeting between James Madison and Old Dominion .
• Of note, all five wins over the Monarchs have come in the Sun Belt era .
• The Dukes also improved to 19-6 against Sun Belt East Division foes since entering the league .
• JMU is now 19-7 on the road since joining the FBS ranks in 2022 .
• The win pushed the Dukes to 4-0 for the sixth time in the last seven seasons .
• JMU scored a touchdown on its opening offensive possession for the fourth straight game to begin the season .
• JMU finished 9-for-9 in the red zone on the night .
• The Dukes’ defense issued nine sacks, tying the program’s FBS record - Oct . 26, 2024 vs . Southern Miss .
• JMU also held ODU to -14 rushing yards on the night, the least since last season’s Sun Belt Championship Game vs . Troy (-26) .

Sept . 19, 2026 | Snapdragon Stadium | San Diego, Calif . | Attendance: 26,487
Dukes Win Road Opener Out West 
SAN DIEGO, Calif. –  In the longest regular-season trip in program history, 
James Madison earned a 26-13 win over San Diego State on Saturday night at 
Snapdragon Stadium .
Quarterback Camden Coleman threw for 246 yards with two touchdowns and 
ran in a third to help the Dukes (3-0) win their road opener for the sixth time 
in the last seven seasons . JMU finished with 417 yards of total offense and 
forced two Aztec turnovers on defense to help secure the win .
JMU got the ball first and went right down the field, taking the early lead on 
a 6-play, 75-yard drive . Coleman scored on a 4-yard touchdown run after he 
connected with wide receiver Braeden Wisloski on a 54-yard reception to get 
the Dukes into the red zone .
Cornerback DJ Kelly picked off a San Diego State pass on the ensuing pos -
session, which led to a 46-yard field goal by kicker Morgan Suarez to make it 
a 10-0 game .
Suarez added his second field goal of the night from 32 yards away to begin the 
second quarter and push JMU’s lead to 13-0 . 
Looking for some momentum, the Aztecs (1-2) recovered a Dukes’ fumble 
midway through the second quarter . But JMU’s defense held, getting a fourth-
down stop at the 3:10 mark .
In the closing moments before halftime, JMU struck again on an opportunistic 
sequence . Linebacker JT Hooten intercepted a pass with 1:02 to play and re -
turned it 14 yards to the SDSU 9-yard line . Two plays later, Coleman found tight 
end Cole Keller for a 7-yard score to make it 19-0 at the break .
The Dukes finished with 281 yards of total offense in the first half .
San Diego State got on the board at the 6:47 mark of the third quarter, when running back Javion Kinnard rushed for a 3-yard score to cut JMU’s lead to 19-7 .
Early in the fourth, Coleman found wide receiver Corey Scott for a key, 20-yard gain on a fourth down, which led to Keller’s second touchdown of the night . 
The Grain Valley, Missouri, native hauled in a 20-yard pass to bump the lead back to 26-7 with 12:54 to play .
Keller finished with three catches for 40 yards and the scores in the win . 
SDSU got within two scores again with a 14-yard touchdown grab by Nathan Acevedo with 8:52 to play . However, the hosts were unable to draw any closer, 
as the JMU defense turned the Aztecs over on downs in each of its last two possessions of the game .
Running back George Pettaway logged 82 rushing yards on 20 carries in the win, while Wisloski finished with five catches for 90 yards . Coleman added 25 
rushing yards on the ground in his second JMU start of his career . 
Defensively, linebacker Nakian Jackson had a game-high 14 tackles, while safety Jayon Harvey tallied 12 of his own . Hooten had six tackles, including a sack, 
to go along with his interception, while defensive lineman Kells Bush also registered a sack for the Dukes .
Saturday marked the first-ever meeting between the Dukes and Aztecs . It also was the squad’s first time in California and second in the Pacific Time Zone . 
With the win, JMU snapped San Diego State’s seven-game, home win streak .
Up next, JMU enters Sun Belt Conference play when it travels to Old Dominion on Saturday, Sept . 26 . Kickoff at S .B . Ballard Stadium in Norfolk is set for 6 
p .m . ET, and the game will be streamed live on ESPN+ .
GAME NOTES
• Saturday marked the first-ever meeting between James Madison and San Diego State .
• With the win, JMU has now won six of its last seven road openers .
• The squad also is now 18-7 on the road since joining the FBS ranks in 2022 .
• That includes a 6-2 mark in road, nonconference matchups in regular season play .
• The win also pushed the Dukes to 5-0 all time against current members of the Pac-12 Conference .
• The Dukes snapped San Diego State’s seven-game home win streak .
• Saturday also marked JMU’s first game played in California and second in the Pacific Time Zone . It was the longest regular-season road trip in program 
history .
• Along with its two turnovers, the JMU defense recorded three stops on fourth downs, including each of SDSU’s last two possessions of the game .
• JMU outgained the Aztecs, 417-401, in the win .
• The Dukes are now 3-0 for the sixth time in the last seven seasons .

Game 2: James Madison 87, Wagner 3
    1 2 3 4 Score 
 Wagner  3 0 0 0 3
 James Madison  21 21 24 21 87
Sept . 12, 2026 | Bridgeforth Stadium | Harrisonburg, Va . | Attendance: 25,028
Dukes Enjoy Record-Setting Day in Wagner Win 
HARRISONBURG, Va. –  James Madison found the end zone 12 times in an 87-3 
victory over Wagner on Saturday afternoon at Bridgeforth Stadium .
It was a record-setting day for the Dukes (2-0), featuring the most points score in 
one game in program history . JMU scored at least 21 points in all four quarters and 
tallied nine touchdowns on 12 offensive drives in the win .
JMU also scored three times on special teams, including a blocked punt return for a 
touchdown and two more punt return for touchdowns .
In all, the Dukes registered 588 yards of total offense on the historic afternoon .
Wide receiver Michael Scott stole the show with 303 all-purpose yards, the fourth-
most in single-game JMU history . The York, Pennsylvania, native started with a 92-
yard kickoff return before he took two punt returns to the house from 76 and 69 
yards, respectively . 
Quarterback Camden Coleman finished 11-of-17 for 170 yards and a touchdown in his 
first JMU start . Quarterback Arrington Maiden added two more touchdown passes in 
second-half relief .
After Wagner (1-2) went three-and-out to open the game, the Dukes’ special teams 
made its presence known early . Wide receiver Miles Halbert blocked a Seahawk punt 
and returned it for a 9-yard touchdown to make it 7-0 .
It marked JMU’s first blocked punt for a touchdown since Sept . 21, 2024, at North 
Carolina .
After Wagner got on the board with a 40-yard field goal on its next possession, Scott 
took the ensuing kickoff 92 yards to set up the Dukes’ offense . Running back George 
Pettaway cashed in with a 1-yard touchdown run to extend the lead to 14-3 . 
Pettaway added his second 1-yard score later in the quarter to make it a 21-3 game .
The fifth-year back made it a hat trick early in the second quarter, scoring on a 32-
yard rush . Pettaway finished with nine carries for 52 yards and the scores .
Running back Nick Herman, who rushed for a game-high 132 yards in the win, found 
the end zone twice . He scored for the first time at James Madison on a 15-yard run in 
the second quarter, before a breakaway, 77-yarder to open the second half .
At the 8:22 mark in the third quarter, Coleman found wide receiver Za’Ron Collins for 
an 18-yard touchdown strike to make it 63-3 . From there, tight end KD Mosley (20-
yard TD) and Halbert (44-yard TD) each caught touchdown passes from Maiden .
Running back Phoenix Donghia finished off the scoring late in the fourth with a 1-yard 
run .
Cornerback Ken Willis led the JMU defense with seven tackles, a sack and fumble 
recovery . In all, the Dukes held Wagner to just 86 rushing yards on 40 attempts, a 
2 .2-yard average .
Along with the special teams scores, kicker Morgan Suarez hit a 46-yard field goal late in the third, his third make on the young season .
Saturday marked the first-ever meeting between James Madison and Wagner . With the win, JMU moved to 5-0 against FCS opponents since joining the FCS ranks in 
2022 . 
The announced attendance on Saturday was 25,028, marking JMU’s eighth straight regular-season sellout . The Dukes have now sold out 18 of their last 20 regu -
lar-season home games .
Next up, the Dukes hit the road for the first time this season with a cross-country trip to San Diego State on Saturday, Sept . 19 . Kickoff is set for 10 p .m . ET, on The CW .
GAME NOTES
• Saturday marked the 20th all-time meeting between James Madison and Liberty .
• JMU’s 87 points were its most in program history .
• Six of JMU’s 12 touchdowns came on the ground, while three each came through the air and on special teams .
• JMU’s blocked punt for a touchdown in the first quarter was its first since Sept . 21, 2024, at North Carolina .
• The Dukes scored 24 points in the third quarter, the most in one frame since Nov . 29, 2025, vs . Coastal Carolina (4Q - 28) .
• With the blocked punt return for a touchdowns and two traditional punt return touchdowns, JMU logged three punt return scores for the first time ever and tied an 
NCAA single-game record .
• JMU scored 21 or more points in all four quarters .
• RB George Pettaway became the first JMU player to score three rushing touchdowns in a game since Jobi Malary on Nov . 15, 2025, vs . App State .
• WR Michael Scott’s 303 all-purpose yards were the fourth-most in JMU history . He is one of three to return two punts for touchdowns .

2026 James Madison Football
Game 1: James Madison 20, Liberty 13
    1 2 3 4 Score 
 Liberty  0 6 0 7 13
 James Madison  7 10 3 0 20
Sept . 5, 2026 | Bridgeforth Stadium | Harrisonburg, Va . | Attendance: 25,035
JMU Opens Season with Battle of the Blue Ridge Victory
 
HARRISONBURG, Va. – James Madison scored points on each of its first four 
drives to earn a 20-13 win over Liberty in the Battle of the Blue Ridge on Sat -
urday at Bridgeforth Stadium .
The Dukes (1-0) never trailed, helping Billy Napier secure his first victory as the 
head coach at JMU . Running back George Pettaway led the way with 15 carries 
for 47 yards and a touchdown, while defensive back Jayon Harvey registered a 
game-high eight tackles on defense .
James Madison opened the year on a 12-play, 80-yard drive, capped by a sev-
en-yard Pettaway touchdown run to make it 7-0 .
After Liberty (0-1) got on the board with a second-quarter field goal, JMU re -
sponded with one of its own . Kicker Morgan Suarez drilled a 46-yard field to 
make it 10-3, before wide receiver Corey Scott caught a five-yard touchdown 
pass from quarterback JC Evans to extend the lead to 17-3 with 1:53 to play 
before halftime .
Evans finished 7-of-9 through the air for 61 yards and the score, while also tally-
ing 69 rushing yards in his first career start .
The Flames made it 17-6 at the break with another field goal on the final play of 
the first half .
Suarez added his second made field goal of the day – a 32-yarder – to open the 
third quarter . It marked his fourth career game at JMU with multiple field goals .
Liberty got within seven (20-13) at the 8:33 mark in the fourth quarter, thanks to a 34-yard touchdown pass from Deshawn Purdie to Makai Jackson . The 
Flames threatened again late but the Dukes’ defense held, forcing a turnover on downs at midfield with 2:16 to play .
From there, JMU was able to run out the clock to preserve the season-opening victory .
With the win, the Dukes improved to 14-6 all time against Liberty, including 7-4 in Harrisonburg . It also marked JMU’s 22nd straight and 102nd overall win 
over an opponent from Virginia .
JMU also extended its streak of home-opening wins to 24, dating back to 2003 .
James Madison finished with just 49 total offensive plays and held a time of possession of just 22:38 .
The announced attendance was 25,035, good for the seventh consecutive regular-season sellout at Bridgeforth Stadium . The Dukes have now sold out 17 
of their last 19 regular-season home games . 
Up next, JMU will remain at home and play host to Wagner on Saturday, Sept . 12, at Bridgeforth Stadium . Kickoff is set for 3:30 p .m . ET .
GAME NOTES
• Saturday marked the 20th all-time meeting between James Madison and Liberty .
• With the win, JMU improved to 14-6 against the Flames, including 7-4 in Harrisonburg .
• The victory also is JMU’s 22nd straight and 102nd overall win over a team from Virginia .
• The Dukes have now won 24 straight home openers at Bridgeforth Stadium, dating back to 2003 .
• Coach Billy Napier earned his first victory as the head coach at James Madison . First-year coaches are now 5-5 in their JMU debuts .
• JMU is now 32-22-1 all time in season openers .
• The Dukes limited Liberty to 91 yards rushing, an average of 2 .4 .
• The Dukes featured 18 players who earned their first start at JMU .
